# Third-party licenses

konoma is distributed under the MIT license (see `LICENSE`). The compiled binary
statically links the following third-party Rust crates. Their license texts are
reproduced below to satisfy attribution requirements for binary distribution.

This file is generated by [`cargo-about`](https://github.com/EmbarkStudios/cargo-about);
regenerate it with `sh licenses/third-party-licenses.sh`.

The crate list comes first. The sections after it cover code, fonts and data that
the license field of a crate does not describe.

## Overview

- MIT License (397)
- Apache License 2.0 (13)
- BSD 3-Clause "New" or "Revised" License (11)
- Unicode License v3 (6)
- ISC License (5)
- Mozilla Public License 2.0 (5)
- BSD 2-Clause "Simplified" License (4)
- zlib License (4)
- Creative Commons Zero v1.0 Universal (1)
- Community Data License Agreement Permissive 2.0 (1)

```
This is the Community Data License Agreement - Permissive, Version
2.0 (the "agreement"). Data Provider(s) and Data Recipient(s) agree
as follows:

available by Data Provider(s) under this agreement if that Data
Recipient follows the terms of this agreement.

1.2. This agreement does not impose any restriction on a Data
Recipient's use, modification, or sharing of any portions of the
Data that are in the public domain or that may be used, modified,
or shared under any other legal exception or limitation.

## 2. Conditions for Sharing Data

2.1. A Data Recipient may share Data, with or without modifications, so
long as the Data Recipient makes available the text of this agreement
with the shared Data.

## 3. No Restrictions on Results

3.1. This agreement does not impose any restriction or obligations
with respect to the use, modification, or sharing of Results.

## 4. No Warranty; Limitation of Liability

4.1. All Data Recipients receive the Data subject to the following
terms:

THE DATA IS PROVIDED ON AN "AS IS" BASIS, WITHOUT REPRESENTATIONS,
WARRANTIES OR CONDITIONS OF ANY KIND, EITHER EXPRESS OR IMPLIED
INCLUDING, WITHOUT LIMITATION, ANY WARRANTIES OR CONDITIONS OF TITLE,
NON-INFRINGEMENT, MERCHANTABILITY OR FITNESS FOR A PARTICULAR PURPOSE.

LIABILITY, WHETHER IN CONTRACT, STRICT LIABILITY, OR TORT (INCLUDING

EVEN IF ADVISED OF THE POSSIBILITY OF SUCH DAMAGES.

## 5. Definitions

5.1. "Data" means the material received by a Data Recipient under
this agreement.

5.2. "Data Provider" means any person who is the source of Data
provided under this agreement and in reliance on a Data Recipient's
agreement to its terms.

5.3. "Data Recipient" means any person who receives Data directly
or indirectly from a Data Provider and agrees to the terms of this
agreement.

5.4. "Results" means any outcome obtained by computational analysis
of Data, including for example machine learning models and models'
insights.
```

## Derived code (not a dependency)

### Markdown renderer, from `tui-markdown` (MIT)

konoma's Markdown renderer (`crates/konoma-widgets/src/markdown/render.rs`) reimplements
`tui_markdown::TextWriter` method-for-method -- the module doc there spells out
the correspondence. `tui-markdown` itself is no longer a Cargo dependency (see
`cargo tree -i tui-markdown`, which resolves to nothing), so `cargo-about` no
longer lists it above. But the reimplementation is still derived code that
konoma distributes, and its MIT license requires the copyright notice below to
travel with it regardless of whether the crate is still linked.

### Embedded KaTeX fonts (SIL Open Font License 1.1)

konoma embeds the KaTeX TrueType faces through `ratex-katex-fonts`, which enables
`ratex-svg`'s `embed-fonts` feature. They typeset LaTeX math, and
`KaTeX_SansSerif-Regular` is also registered as the last-resort `sans-serif` face
for mermaid diagrams, so a machine with no system fonts -- a slim container, a CI
runner -- still draws them.

`cargo-about` reports that crate's declared license, which is MIT and covers its
Rust code. **The font binaries it ships are separately licensed under the SIL Open
Font License 1.1**, as `fonts/OFL.txt` and `fonts/FONT_NOTICE.txt` inside the crate
state; the faces come from the KaTeX project (https://github.com/KaTeX/KaTeX).
Because the distinction is inside the crate rather than in its metadata, the
dependency graph cannot express it and this notice is maintained by hand.

The faces are embedded unmodified. OFL 1.1 permits bundling them in a larger work,
including a commercial one, and konoma does not sell them on their own or use the
Reserved Font Name for a modified version.

The copyright notice of the faces, as recorded in the `name` table (name ID 0) of
every one of the TrueType files:

```
Copyright (c) 2009-2010 Design Science, Inc.
Copyright (c) 2014-2018 Khan Academy
```

The license statement in the same table (name ID 13), shown here as
`KaTeX_AMS-Regular.ttf` records it:

```
Copyright (c) 2009-2010, Design Science, Inc. (<www.mathjax.org>)
Copyright (c) 2014-2018 Khan Academy (<www.khanacademy.org>),
with Reserved Font Name KaTeX_AMS.

This Font Software is licensed under the SIL Open Font License, Version 1.1.
This license available with a FAQ at:
http://scripts.sil.org/OFL
```

The other families record the same statement with their own Reserved Font Name:
KaTeX_Caligraphic, KaTeX_Fraktur, KaTeX_Main, KaTeX_Math, KaTeX_SansSerif,
KaTeX_Script, KaTeX_Size1, KaTeX_Size2, KaTeX_Size3, KaTeX_Size4 and
KaTeX_Typewriter.

The license text, as shipped at `fonts/OFL.txt` in `ratex-katex-fonts`:

### Mermaid layout engine, from `dagre` (Apache-2.0)

konoma's mermaid renderer lays diagrams out with a vendored copy of the `dagre`
crate, version 0.1.1 (https://github.com/kookyleo/dagre-rs, archived). The source
lives in `konoma-mermaid-core/src/layout/` and ships inside the konoma-mermaid-core crate; it is not
a Cargo dependency, because `[patch.crates-io]` is stripped by `cargo publish` and
konoma has to be able to fix the layout engine in a released build. `cargo-about`
walks the dependency graph, so it cannot see this code -- the notice below is
maintained by hand.

The vendored files were modified by the konoma authors (edition-2024 let-chains
desugared for konoma's edition 2021, module paths rewritten, unused graph algorithms
dropped). Every file says so in its header and
`konoma-mermaid-core/src/layout/PROVENANCE.md` lists each change, as Apache-2.0 section
4(b) requires. Upstream ships no `NOTICE` file, so there is none to reproduce here.
The full license text is in `konoma-mermaid-core/src/layout/LICENSE-APACHE` and is also
reproduced above under "Apache License 2.0".

```
Copyright (c) kookyleo <kookyleo@gmail.com>

Licensed under the Apache License, Version 2.0 (the "License");
you may not use this file except in compliance with the License.
You may obtain a copy of the License at

    http://www.apache.org/licenses/LICENSE-2.0

Unless required by applicable law or agreed to in writing, software
distributed under the License is distributed on an "AS IS" BASIS,
WITHOUT WARRANTIES OR CONDITIONS OF ANY KIND, either express or implied.
See the License for the specific language governing permissions and
limitations under the License.
```

### Mermaid layout engine, from `dagre.js` and `graphlib` (MIT)

The `dagre` crate vendored above is itself a Rust port of the JavaScript library
`@dagrejs/dagre` (version 3.0.1-pre, commit `4713b59bfa05af56cf58aa01e2027adf5d2dcf88`;
https://github.com/dagrejs/dagre), and its graph data structure is a port of
`graphlib` (https://github.com/dagrejs/graphlib; that dagre commit depends on version
4.0.1). The derived code is in `konoma-mermaid-core/src/layout/layout/` (from dagre.js, except
`intersect.rs`, which ports `dagre-d3-es`; see the next section),
`konoma-mermaid-core/src/layout/mod.rs`, `src/layout/util.rs`,
`src/layout/cross_validate.rs` and `src/layout/cross-validate/` (the vendored crate's
module, helper and comparison with dagre.js), and
`konoma-mermaid-core/src/layout/graph/` (from graphlib).

### Mermaid diagram readers and drawing rules, from `mermaid` (MIT)

Parts of `konoma-mermaid-core` were written by reading the source of `mermaid`
(https://github.com/mermaid-js/mermaid) and reproduce its grammar, rules, formulas and
values; some are ported or transcribed directly. The files cite tags such as
`mermaid@11.12.0` and `mermaid@11.17.2`, whose `LICENSE` files are identical.

- grammar, parsing and diagram database rules: `src/architecture.rs`, `src/block.rs`,
  `src/c4.rs`, `src/gantt.rs`, `src/gantt/time.rs` (`ganttDb`'s date and duration
  handling), `src/gitgraph.rs`, `src/journey.rs`, `src/kanban.rs`, `src/mindmap.rs`,
  `src/requirement.rs`, `src/timeline.rs`, and the files under `src/chart/`,
  `src/class/`, `src/er/`, `src/flowchart/`, `src/sequence/` and `src/state/`
- edge corner handling (`fixCorners`, `generateRoundedPath`):
  `src/render/edges.rs`
- shape sizing formulas, drawing rules and defaults: `src/render/shapes.rs`,
  `src/render/mod.rs`, `src/render/state.rs`, `src/render/clusters.rs`,
  `src/render/panel.rs`, `src/render/sequence.rs`, `src/render/labels.rs`,
  `src/render/style.rs`, `src/render/chart/radar.rs`; other files under `src/render/`
  also follow how `mermaid` draws a diagram
- color values of `mermaid`'s themes: `src/render/theme.rs`


### Preset shape definitions (Ecma International, ECMA-376)

konoma draws the PowerPoint/OpenDocument preset shapes from `presetShapeDefinitions.xml`,
the machine-readable definition of the DrawingML preset geometries published with
ECMA-376 Part 1 (5th edition, December 2016;
https://ecma-international.org/wp-content/uploads/ECMA-376-1_5th_edition_december_2016.zip).
The file is embedded **unmodified** at `konoma-office-core/src/slide_draw/presets/presetShapeDefinitions.xml`
and parsed at run time; it is not a Cargo dependency, so `cargo-about` cannot see it and
this notice is maintained by hand. **It is not under konoma's MIT licence**; it stays under
the Ecma copyright licence below, which is also kept next to the file in
`konoma-office-core/src/slide_draw/presets/NOTICE.md`.

```

© Ecma International

However, the content of this document itself may not be modified in any way, including by removing the copyright notice or references to Ecma International, except as required to translate it into languages other than English or into a different format.
```

### Preset pattern bitmaps (OpenPresentation, MIT)

The 54 8 x 8 bitmaps of the DrawingML preset patterns (`a:pattFill`) in
`konoma-office-core/src/slide_draw/patterns.rs` are the tiles measured from desktop PowerPoint and
published as `pattern-fills.ts` in https://github.com/OpenPresentation/opf (MIT licence). The
Wingdings 2 and Webdings code tables in `konoma-office-core/src/slide_draw/symbol_font.rs` were
read from the Unicode document L2/11-052 (https://www.unicode.org/L2/L2011/11052-wingding.pdf)
and are konoma's own transcription.

```text
The bundled PCRE implementation (deps/pcre2/) is licensed under the
PCRE2 Licence.

PCRE2 is a library of functions to support regular expressions whose syntax
and semantics are as close as possible to those of the Perl 5 language.

Releases 10.00 and above of PCRE2 are distributed under the terms of the "BSD"
licence, as specified below, with one exemption for certain binary
redistributions. The documentation for PCRE2, supplied in the "doc" directory,
is distributed under the same terms as the software itself. The data in the
testdata directory is not copyrighted and is in the public domain.

The basic library functions are written in C and are freestanding. Also
included in the distribution is a just-in-time compiler that can be used to
optimize pattern matching. This is an optional feature that can be omitted when
the library is built. The just-in-time compiler is separately licensed under the
"2-clause BSD" licence.
```

`libgit2/deps/pcre2/LICENCE.md`:

```text
PCRE2 Licence
=============

| SPDX-License-Identifier: | BSD-3-Clause WITH PCRE2-exception |
|---------|-------|

PCRE2 is a library of functions to support regular expressions whose syntax
and semantics are as close as possible to those of the Perl 5 language.

Releases 10.00 and above of PCRE2 are distributed under the terms of the "BSD"
licence, as specified below, with one exemption for certain binary
redistributions. The documentation for PCRE2, supplied in the "doc" directory,
is distributed under the same terms as the software itself. The data in the
testdata directory is not copyrighted and is in the public domain.

The basic library functions are written in C and are freestanding. Also
included in the distribution is a just-in-time compiler that can be used to
optimize pattern matching. This is an optional feature that can be omitted when
the library is built. The just-in-time compiler is separately licensed under the
"2-clause BSD" licence.


COPYRIGHT
---------

### The basic library functions

    Written by:       Philip Hazel
    Email local part: Philip.Hazel
    Email domain:     gmail.com

    Retired from University of Cambridge Computing Service,
    Cambridge, England.

    Copyright (c) 1997-2007 University of Cambridge
    Copyright (c) 2007-2024 Philip Hazel
    All rights reserved.

### PCRE2 Just-In-Time compilation support

    Written by:       Zoltan Herczeg
```

## Adobe Core 14 font metrics

The glyph widths and the Standard, Symbol and ZapfDingbats encodings of the 14 standard PDF fonts, converted from Adobe's AFM files into Rust tables (a modified form of the files). The paragraph is from `MustRead.html`, which accompanies the AFM files in `assets/font_metrics/` of LaurenzV/hayro; the lines after it are the notices of the files.

Found in:

- `hayro-interpret` 0.8.0: `src/font/generated/metrics.rs`
- `hayro-interpret` 0.8.0: `src/font/generated/standard.rs`
- `hayro-interpret` 0.8.0: `src/font/generated/symbol.rs`
- `hayro-interpret` 0.8.0: `src/font/generated/zapf_dings.rs`

## Independent JPEG Group (`image` 0.25.10)

In this program, this software is based in part on the work of the Independent JPEG Group. The JPEG encoder's forward DCT (`src/codecs/jpeg/transform.rs`) is a Rust translation of `jfdctint.c` from libjpeg 9a.

```text
/*
fdct is a Rust translation of jfdctint.c from the
Independent JPEG Group's libjpeg version 9a
obtained from http://www.ijg.org/files/jpegsr9a.zip
It comes with the following conditions of distribution and use:

    In plain English:

    1. We don't promise that this software works.  (But if you find any bugs,
        please let us know!)
    2. You can use this software for whatever you want.  You don't have to pay us.
    3. You may not pretend that you wrote this software.  If you use it in a
       program, you must acknowledge somewhere in your documentation that
       you've used the IJG code.

    In legalese:

    The authors make NO WARRANTY or representation, either express or implied,
    with respect to this software, its quality, accuracy, merchantability, or
    fitness for a particular purpose.  This software is provided "AS IS", and you,
    its user, assume the entire risk as to its quality and accuracy.

    This software is copyright (C) 1991-2014, Thomas G. Lane, Guido Vollbeding.
    All Rights Reserved except as specified below.

    Permission is hereby granted to use, copy, modify, and distribute this
    software (or portions thereof) for any purpose, without fee, subject to these
    conditions:
    (1) If any part of the source code for this software is distributed, then this
    README file must be included, with this copyright and no-warranty notice
    unaltered; and any additions, deletions, or changes to the original files
    must be clearly indicated in accompanying documentation.
    (2) If only executable code is distributed, then the accompanying
    documentation must state that "this software is based in part on the work of
    the Independent JPEG Group".
    (3) Permission for use of this software is granted only if the user accepts
    full responsibility for any undesirable consequences; the authors accept
    NO LIABILITY for damages of any kind.

    These conditions apply to any software derived from or based on the IJG code,
    not just to the unmodified library.  If you use our work, you ought to
    acknowledge us.

    Permission is NOT granted for the use of any IJG author's name or company name
    in advertising or publicity relating to this software or products derived from
    it.  This software may be referred to only as "the Independent JPEG Group's
    software".

    We specifically permit and encourage the use of this software as the basis of
    commercial products, provided that all warranty or liability claims are
    assumed by the product vendor.
```

## Blur filters of resvg

`box_blur.rs` is based on fastblur. `iir_blur.rs` is based on Pascal Getreuer's `gaussianiir`, and the comment below is what `resvg` records about its license.

Found in:

- `resvg` 0.48.1: `src/filter/box_blur.rs`
- `resvg` 0.48.1: `src/filter/iir_blur.rs`

From `resvg`, `src/filter/iir_blur.rs`:

```text
// An IIR blur.
//
// Based on http://www.getreuer.info/home/gaussianiir
//
// Licensed under 'Simplified BSD License'.
//
//
// Implements the fast Gaussian convolution algorithm of Alvarez and Mazorra,
// where the Gaussian is approximated by a cascade of first-order infinite
// impulsive response (IIR) filters.  Boundaries are handled with half-sample
// symmetric extension.
//
// Gaussian convolution is approached as approximating the heat equation and
// each timestep is performed with an efficient recursive computation.  Using
// more steps yields a more accurate approximation of the Gaussian.  A
// reasonable default value for `numsteps` is 4.
//
// Reference:
// Alvarez, Mazorra, "Signal and Image Restoration using Shock Filters and
// Anisotropic Diffusion," SIAM J. on Numerical Analysis, vol. 31, no. 2,
// pp. 590-605, 1994.
```

## Adler-32 code from Chromium zlib

The NEON implementation, used on AArch64.

Found in:

- `simd-adler32` 0.3.10: `src/imp/neon.rs`

Header of `third_party/zlib/adler32_simd.c`, chromium/chromium 6d765ed98aed:

```text
/* adler32_simd.c
 *
 * Copyright 2017 The Chromium Authors
 * Use of this source code is governed by a BSD-style license that can be
 * found in the Chromium source repository LICENSE file.
 *
 * Per http://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adler-32 the adler32 A value (aka s1) is
 * the sum of N input data bytes D1 ... DN,
 *
 *   A = A0 + D1 + D2 + ... + DN
 *
 * where A0 is the initial value.
 *
 * SSE2 _mm_sad_epu8() can be used for byte sums (see http://bit.ly/2wpUOeD,
 * for example) and accumulating the byte sums can use SSE shuffle-adds (see
 * the "Integer" section of http://bit.ly/2erPT8t for details). Arm NEON has
 * similar instructions.
 *
 * The adler32 B value (aka s2) sums the A values from each step:
 *
 *   B0 + (A0 + D1) + (A0 + D1 + D2) + ... + (A0 + D1 + D2 + ... + DN) or
 *
 *       B0 + N.A0 + N.D1 + (N-1).D2 + (N-2).D3 + ... + (N-(N-1)).DN
 *
 * B0 being the initial value. For 32 bytes (ideal for garden-variety SIMD):
 *
 *   B = B0 + 32.A0 + [D1 D2 D3 ... D32] x [32 31 30 ... 1].
 *
 * Adjacent blocks of 32 input bytes can be iterated with the expressions to
 * compute the adler32 s1 s2 of M >> 32 input bytes [1].
 *
 * As M grows, the s1 s2 sums grow. If left unchecked, they would eventually
 * overflow the precision of their integer representation (bad). However, s1
 * and s2 also need to be computed modulo the adler BASE value (reduced). If
 * at most NMAX bytes are processed before a reduce, s1 s2 _cannot_ overflow
 * a uint32_t type (the NMAX constraint) [2].
 *
 * [1] the iterative equations for s2 contain constant factors; these can be
 * hoisted from the n-blocks do loop of the SIMD code.
 *
 * [2] zlib adler32_z() uses this fact to implement NMAX-block-based updates
 * of the adler s1 s2 of uint32_t type (see adler32.c).
 */
```

## KaTeX code and data in the RaTeX crates (MIT)

## mhchem for KaTeX, in `ratex-parser`

`ratex-parser` is described by its own source as a port of KaTeX mhchem 3.3.0, and embeds
data generated from it (`src/mhchem/data/*.json`). The header comment of
`contrib/mhchem/mhchem.js` at the same KaTeX commit follows. The full Apache License 2.0
text it refers to appears in the crate license list above.

```text
/*************************************************************
 *
 *  KaTeX mhchem.js
 *
 *  This file implements a KaTeX version of mhchem version 3.3.0.
 *  It is adapted from MathJax/extensions/TeX/mhchem.js
 *  It differs from the MathJax version as follows:
 *    1. The interface is changed so that it can be called from KaTeX, not MathJax.
 *    2. \rlap and \llap are replaced with \mathrlap and \mathllap.
 *    3. Four lines of code are edited in order to use \raisebox instead of \raise.
 *    4. The reaction arrow code is simplified. All reaction arrows are rendered
 *       using KaTeX extensible arrows instead of building non-extensible arrows.
 *    5. \tripledash vertical alignment is slightly adjusted.
 *
 *    This code, as other KaTeX code, is released under the MIT license.
 * 
 * /*************************************************************
 *
 *  MathJax/extensions/TeX/mhchem.js
 *
 *  Implements the \ce command for handling chemical formulas
 *  from the mhchem LaTeX package.
 *
 *  ---------------------------------------------------------------------
 *
 *  Copyright (c) 2011-2015 The MathJax Consortium
```
